    +def _scheduler_forwards(timing: dict, recs: dict[str, dict], deltas: list[dict[str, tuple[int, int, int | None]]], ran: list[bool],
    +                        lag: int, resolved_lag: int | None, problems: list[str]) -> tuple[list[bool], int, dict[str, Any] | None]:
    +    """The forward map and the lag `attribute` goes on with, and the correction it records, from the scheduler's `deltas` (one per
    +    iteration).  `ran` and `lag` are the arrival loop's inference.  The scheduler's own map and lag are returned, and where its exact
    +    values differ from the loop's they are written to `timing` and `recs` (the correction, applied).  Only where the derived lag
    +    contradicts `resolved_lag` is nothing written: the loop's `ran` and `lag` are returned and the contradiction is added to
    +    `problems` (see `attribute`)."""

    +
    +The event log (`settings.record_event`; `$AGENT_GUARD_EVENT_LOG`, default `/tmp/agent-guard-events.jsonl`) holds one line
    +for each run of the shell, Task and stop hooks: when, which hook, the event's name, its conversation id and tool name, the
    +payload's key names, and of the payload's other values only `model` and `generation_id` (the turn's id), each where the
    +payload has it as a string, cut at 64 characters; never the command, a path or an address. Past about 1 MB it is renamed
    +to `.1` and started again. `inbox.py check`, `selftest` and `guard` read from it whether a shell hook has run for an agent,
    +and `inbox.py turns` the model each of an agent's turns names there. Each VM's log is its own.

tools/agent-guard/shell_hook.py: edit beyond moved paths and module names
    @@ -35,0 +35,5 @@
    +Each reads a command, typed or carried, as the shell runs it (`words_of`): a comment hides the rest of its own line and
    +nothing after it, a `#` inside a word or a quoted string (`issue#12`, a URL's fragment) hides nothing, and a `\\` at a line's
    +end joins the next line to it. Quotes are what shlex takes for them, so three things are read otherwise than the shell reads
    +them: a comment inside backticks, a quote nested in a `$(...)` inside another, and a `$'...'` that holds an escaped quote.
    +

    +
    +A commit made here is the clone's own (`identity_env`: `sync`'s and its rebase's, the archive's, `snapshot`'s): where the
    +configuration git reads in the clone names an author and a committer, git is not handed the calling shell's GIT_AUTHOR_NAME,

    +# directory and renames the link onto it: what runs from the tree resolves the link once, when it starts (`readlink -e`), and
    +# so never runs files of two commits. Such a tree is a copy that may be behind `main` until its next install, and all it
    +# differs from `main` by is that one thing: its stale files, the files `main` has added since and those `main` has dropped
    +# (`dropped` in drift, never a stray: `attic` refuses every path inside the tree, and the next install drops them). The `drift`
    +# monitor keeps them out of the node's incidents and opens the copy's own once every reading for a week has found that copy
    +# behind. A file of the copy that a branch or no commit has is the node's `ahead` or `unknown`, at once.
